Lawmakers want answers from CDC director on childhood vaccine recommendations, measles response News brief Chris Dall, MA Topics Childhood Vaccines Measles US Coast Guard, Nicole Foguth / Flickr A letter to Centers for Disease Control and Prevention (CDC) director Erica Schwartz, MD, MPH, JD, from Democratic lawmakers indicates a growing frustration with her leadership amid attempts by President Trump and Health Secretary Robert F. Kennedy Jr. to change childhood vaccine recommendations. In a letter sent earlier this week, Sen. Michael Bennet (D-CO), Rep. Kim Schrier (D-Wash.) and 29 Democratic colleagues asked Schwartz whether she or any CDC career scientists were consulted about or played a role in developing President Trump's August 10th executive order (EO), which cut the number of recommended childhood vaccines from 18 to 11 and called for the measles, mumps, and rubella (MMR) vaccine to be broken up into three separate shots. Following the order, the Department of Health and Human Services posted a request for information (RFI) aimed at laying the groundwork for the changes. Citing Schwartz's comments at her confirmation hearing, where she pledged to "never betray the science," the lawmakers challenged the director about the scientific basis behind the EO and asked if and how the CDC plans to implement it. "As CDC Director, you bear a responsibility to uphold scientific integrity, and we write seeking answers on how the CDC will respond to the EO and the RFI, and how you will honor the pledge you made to ensure that the CDC leads with evidence-based public health," the letter states. Questions about measles response Bennet and his colleagues called into question the CDC's handling of measles deaths in Pennsylvania and asked whether the agency has instituted a new policy for handling state-reported health data. While Pennsylvania officials have identified five measles-associated deaths, the CDC currently only acknowledges two deaths. "If the agency under your leadership is altering verified state data to fit political preferences, the pledge you made at your confirmation hearing has already been broken," they wrote. The letter also asks Schwartz to explain why the CDC delayed opening orders for COVID vaccines through the Vaccines for Children program, which provides vaccines for low-income children. The lawmakers asked Schwartz for written responses by October 16th. B 常规重要公共卫生新闻

在亚基马县的两个奶牛场检测到 禽流感

Avian influenza detected at two Yakima County dairy farms
新闻简报在华盛顿州亚基马县的两个农场的奶牛中检测到了 禽流感 A 病毒,特别是 H5N1。据亚基马卫生区 2026 年 10 月 8 日报道,这标志着该州首例牛 禽流感 确诊病例。农业部国家兽医服务实验室和华盛顿州农业部证实了这一检测。受影响的农场已接受隔离,并已实施严格的生物安全措施。虽然亚基马县没有确诊的人类病例,但卫生官员警告说,该病毒可以通过与受感染的动物或受污染的环境的密切接触传播给人类。风险较高的人包括畜牧业工人、后院羊群主人、猎人和野生动物生物学家。为了降低风险,官员建议避免食用生牛奶和其他生乳制品,并采取额外的预防措施,例如为那些与工作相关的接触者佩戴个人防护装备。巴氏杀菌奶制品对消费者来说仍然是安全的,预计不会出现牛奶供应短缺。公众面临的风险被认为很低,但卫生官员正在监测暴露的个人并提供保护公众健康的指导。
查看新闻五要素
时间
亚基马卫生区报告于 2026 年 10 月 8 日星期四在华盛顿州的牛中首次检测到 禽流感。该文章于 1 小时前更新。
地点与对象
该事件发生在华盛顿州亚基马县的两个奶牛场。受影响的人群包括接触受感染牛的人,以及畜牧业工人、后院羊群主人、猎人和野生动物生物学家。
事件
在亚基马县的两个农场的奶牛中检测到 禽流感 A 病毒。亚基马卫生区证实了这一检测,农业部国家兽医服务实验室和华盛顿州农业部也证实了H5N1检测。
规模、影响与风险
自 2024 年 3 月以来,暴发 已感染 19 个州的奶牛。目前亚基马县没有确诊的人间病例。公众面临的风险仍然很低,但风险较高的人包括畜牧业工人、后院羊群主人、猎人和野生动物工作人员。该病毒可以通过与受感染的动物或受污染的环境密切接触而传播给人类。
应对、状态与不确定性
两个受影响的农场已接受隔离,并已实施严格的生物安全措施。卫生官员正在监测接触者并提供指导以保护公众健康。巴氏灭菌奶制品被认为是安全的,但生奶和其他生乳制品是不安全的。官员建议避免饮用生牛奶,并采取预防措施,例如为那些与工作相关的接触者穿戴个人防护装备。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Avian influenza detected at two Yakima County dairy farms

News Brief
Avian influenza A viruses, specifically H5N1, have been detected in dairy cattle at two farms in Yakima County, Washington state. This marks the first confirmed case of avian influenza in cattle in the state, as reported by the Yakima Health District on October 8, 2026. The Department of Agriculture National Veterinary Services Laboratory and the Washington State Department of Agriculture confirmed the detection. The affected farms are under quarantine and have implemented strict biosecurity measures. While there are no confirmed human cases in Yakima County, health officials warn that the virus can spread to humans through close contact with infected animals or contaminated environments. Those at higher risk include animal agricultural workers, backyard flock owners, hunters, and wildlife biologists. To reduce the risk, officials advise avoiding raw milk and other raw dairy products, and taking additional precautions such as wearing PPE for those with job-related exposures. Pasteurized milk products remain safe for consumers, and there are no expected milk supply shortages. The risk to the general public is considered low, but health officials are monitoring exposed individuals and providing guidance to protect public health.
Original source text (English)

Featured Spotlight Avian influenza detected at two Yakima County dairy farms TAMMY AYER Yakima Herald-Republic Tammy Ayer Features/The Vanished Editor Author facebook Author twitter Author email Oct 8, 2026 Oct 8, 2026 Updated 1 hr ago 0 Facebook Twitter WhatsApp SMS Email Facebook Twitter WhatsApp SMS Email Print Copy article link Save The first detection of avian influenza in cattle in Washington state has been confirmed at two dairy farms in Yakima County, the Yakima Health District reported Thursday. The U.S. Department of Agriculture National Veterinary Services Laboratory and the Washington State Department of Agriculture confirmed the H5N1 detection, according to a news release from the health district. The health district is working with the two dairies to monitor and protect the health of people exposed to the infected cattle, it said. The dairies weren't identified. Dairy cattle in 19 states have been infected since the outbreak began in March 2024, the release said. There are currently no confirmed human cases of avian influenza, commonly called bird flu, in Yakima County. Avian influenza is caused by Type A influenza viruses that occur naturally in wild birds. The virus can spread from wild birds to other animals, including poultry and mammals such as dairy cattle. Human infections with avian influenza viruses are rare, the release said. “The risk to the general public remains low," Dr. Dave Atteberry, health officer for the health district, said in the release. Pasteurized milk products remain safe for consumers, according to a news release from the state agriculture department, and milk supply shortages aren't expected. Officials: Avoid raw milk Infected cows can shed the virus in their milk, making raw milk and other raw dairy products unsafe for consumption, county health officials said. Avoid raw dairy product consumption and don't allow pets to consume raw milk or other dairy products, or raw foods such as raw meat. The two Yakima County farms that are impacted are under quarantine and have employed strict biosecurity measures, a news release from the state agriculture department said. Veterinarians are treating sick cows. The county health district "is working closely with dairy producers, workers, and our local, state, and federal partners to provide guidance and resources to protect the health of people who may have been exposed to the infected cattle," Atteberry said. Though there is no evidence of human-to-human transmission at this time, avian influenza can spread to humans following close contact with infected animals or contaminated environments, state agriculture and county health officials said. Those who may be at higher risk include animal agricultural workers, backyard flock owners, hunters and people who work with wildlife, such as wildlife biologists. Taking precautions The health district can help coordinate testing for any exposed person who develops symptoms to ensure prompt identification and appropriate treatment for any human cases. To reduce the risk of avian influenza, don't touch wild birds or animals. Wild birds and other animals can carry avian influenza even if they appear healthy. Avoid contact with sick or dead birds or animals, and with bird droppings or contaminated surfaces. People with job-related exposures to infected birds or other infected animals or their environments should take additional precautions, including wearing recommended PPE. Those who suspect illness in cattle or backyard birds should use extra precautions. Hunters should practice good hygiene when handling game. Wear disposable gloves when cleaning harvested birds or cleaning bird feeders. And though the seasonal flu vaccine doesn't prevent bird flu, it reduces the risk of co-infection with human and avian influenza viruses, county health officials said. B 常规重要公共卫生新闻

鸟类病毒如何感染人? BC省人类感染H5N1禽流感病例的惊人发现 - Daily Nation

How does a bird virus infect a person? Surprising findings from B.C.’s human case of H5N1 avian flu - Daily Nation
新闻简报Daily Nation 最近的一篇文章讨论了 2024 年在加拿大不列颠哥伦比亚省确诊的一例 H5N1 禽流感人类病例。该患者是一名 13 岁的患者,经历了严重的呼吸衰竭,但经过重症监护后康复了。研究人员分析了该案例中的病毒突变,发现了意想不到的结果。血凝素(一种关键的表面蛋白)中的 突变 不支持与人型受体的结合,并且与禽类受体的结合严重减少。然而,该病毒仍然在人体组织中复制,表明 传播 具有人畜共患的可能性。该研究强调需要进一步研究,以了解此类 突变 如何影响病毒感染人类的​​能力以及对公共卫生准备的更广泛影响。这些发现基于实验室进行的病毒学测试,包括测序和受体结合分析。文章强调了监测流感 暴发 和了解物种适应机制的重要性。
查看新闻五要素
时间
该文章发表于2026年10月4日。证据中没有明确说明事件日期,但人感染H5N1禽流感病例于2024年得到证实。
地点与对象
该病例发生在加拿大不列颠哥伦比亚省,涉及一名十三岁患者。受影响人群包括接触 H5N1 禽流感 的人类。
事件
2024 年,加拿大不列颠哥伦比亚省确认了一例人感染 H5N1 禽流感病例。患者是一名 13 岁的患者,患有严重的呼吸衰竭,但经过重症监护后康复了。该研究分析了这种情况下的病毒 突变,并发现了有关受体结合和细胞融合的意外结果。
规模、影响与风险
该病例是一起人类感染 H5N1 禽流感的病例。研究发现,血凝素中的突变不支持与人型受体的结合,与禽类受体的结合严重减弱。然而,该病毒仍然在人体组织中复制,表明 传播 具有人畜共患的可能性。
应对、状态与不确定性
该病例已得到公共卫生机构的确认。该研究由实验室进行,研究结果基于病毒学测试,包括测序和受体结合分析。结果表明需要进一步研究以了解人畜共患 传播 的机制以及对未来 暴发 的影响。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

How does a bird virus infect a person? Surprising findings from B.C.’s human case of H5N1 avian flu - Daily Nation

News Brief
A recent article from Daily Nation discusses a human case of H5N1 avian flu confirmed in British Columbia, Canada, in 2024. The patient, a thirteen-year-old, experienced severe respiratory failure but recovered with intensive care. Researchers analyzed the viral mutations in this case and found unexpected results. The mutations in hemagglutinin, a key surface protein, did not support binding to human-type receptors, and binding to avian receptors was severely reduced. However, the virus still replicated in human tissue, suggesting potential for zoonotic transmission. The study highlights the need for further research to understand how such mutations might influence the virus's ability to infect humans and the broader implications for public health preparedness. The findings are based on virological tests, including sequencing and receptor binding analysis, conducted by a lab. The article emphasizes the importance of monitoring influenza outbreaks and understanding the mechanisms of species adaptation.
Original source text (English)

Reclaim your full access. Click below to renew. Renew subscription Maybe later Subscribe for a month to get full access How does a bird virus infect a person? Surprising findings from B.C.’s human case of H5N1 avian flu Sunday, October 04, 2026 Avian flu (bird flu) is a contagious viral disease caused by Avian Influenza Type A viruses that primarily affects wild aquatic birds and domestic poultry. Photo credit: File By The Conversation What you need to know: Canada's first human H5N1 infection was a medical mystery: the virus shouldn't have bound well to human cells, yet it caused severe respiratory failure. New research suggests there is more to learn about what makes a virus dangerous. How do we know when an animal virus might pose a risk to humans? Canada confirmed a human case of H5N1 avian influenza (often called “bird flu”) in 2024, the first ever known to have been acquired in Canada. The patient, a thirteen-year-old British Columbian, suffered severe symptoms including respiratory failure but recovered with intensive care in hospital. Uncovering insights into how pathogens can switch from animals to humans — as in this human case of bird flu — can help researchers and public health agencies prepare for future outbreaks. In a recent study, our lab examined viral mutations in this case and uncovered an unexpected result: the effects of these mutations were inconsistent with the usual warning signs we expect to see in a virus adapted to infect humans. How we usually monitor influenza Public health agencies and collaborating groups use complementary approaches to monitor infectious disease outbreaks and risk. Human outbreak data comes from many places, including care settings and associated surveillance labs, volunteers reporting disease status, and wastewater monitoring. Current routine virological tests in public health labs include sequencing viral genes, testing for antiviral resistance and assessing immunity against strains of interest. The other two mutations caught our attention since they are less understood. Both were in hemagglutinin, the viral surface protein that binds to cells and initiates infection by fusing the virus and cell membranes. These mutations, E190D and Q226H (representing 28 per cent and 35 per cent, respectively, of hemagglutinin genetic material sequenced), were at the part of hemagglutinin known to interact with receptors on host cells. Bird and human cells tend to display different types of receptors, which is why scientists use receptor binding as a common test for species adaptation. These hemagglutinin mutations had not previously been observed to switch binding to human-type receptors in H5N1 influenza. What this case revealed Our lab investigated the role of these two mutations in what types of receptors these viruses bind to more favourably. Receptor binding can influence which cells, and which species, a virus is more likely to infect. Therefore, we made versions of the hemagglutinin protein with and without each mutation and measured how they bound to different receptors. The results were unexpected. When we produced hemagglutinin protein with either of these mutations, not only did we not observe the expected binding to human-type receptors that would support human infection, but the binding to avian receptors was severely diminished. Using standard approaches, binding to any receptor was essentially undetectable. Yet the virus in the B.C. patient clearly replicated in human tissue. So, our team tested the ability of the hemagglutinin variant carrying both mutations to fuse cells derived from human lung cells. After binding to host cells, fusion is the second major step mediated by hemagglutinin. The result of fusion is that viral genetic material and proteins required for replication are released from inside the viral particle into the host cell where they can function. Just as we saw when we looked at the receptor binding, we saw a substantial reduction in the virus’s hemagglutinin protein’s ability to fuse membranes of the lung-derived cells, likely a consequence of the reduced binding. However, we did detect a low level of fusion, which suggests that we might see both receptor binding and membrane fusion activity under conditions that better mimic actual human lungs. In a human case of infection with a virus that usually affects birds, we would normally expect to see the surface protein (hemagglutinin) binding with human receptors. That would be typical in a virus that was adapting from birds to humans. But what we saw in the B.C. case of human infection with H5N1 was a reduced ability not only to bind to human receptors, but also to bird cell receptors, resulting in a low ability to fuse with cell membranes. However, the combination of lots of hemagglutinin proteins on the virus’s surface, and many receptors on the cell’s surface, may have compensated for the poor ability to bind to individual receptors. What we can learn from this case This case demonstrates that there is much to learn about the complex factors that may result in a virus developing the ability to infect humans. From decades of investigation, we had reason to believe these hemagglutinin mutations were interesting, so it’s a success for our existing knowledge base. Our lab depended on successes in the public health system leading up to the B.C. Centre for Disease Control Public Health Laboratory being able to sequence viruses in the patient, and releasing its data to the influenza research community, as well as generous support of our research from the federal and provincial sources. The unexpected findings suggest that we need greater functional understanding of human disease risk. Our existing set of mutational warning signs does not capture all threats as we monitor animal outbreaks. Functional laboratory studies are slower and more expensive, but they can tell us more directly how viruses behave. Public health agencies already run certain on-the-fly functional tests such as assessing vaccine match to seasonal influenza. This data continues to inform our response to outbreaks. Greater monitoring of other influenza functions would further enhance public health efforts. The stronger our basic knowledge of how these viruses work, the better we can interpret surveillance data, prepare for outbreaks and design interventions. -John Ni, PhD Candidate in Biochemistry and Molecular Biology, University of British Columbia,and Sriram Subramaniam, Professor, Faculty of Medicine, University of British Columbia Login to join the discussion Please enable JavaScript to view the comments powered by Disqus. The article was published on October 4, 2026. The event date is not explicitly stated in the evidence, but the human case of H5N1 avian flu was confirmed in 2024.
Location and population
The case occurred in British Columbia, Canada, involving a thirteen-year-old patient. The affected population includes humans exposed to H5N1 avian influenza.
Event
A human case of H5N1 avian flu was confirmed in British Columbia, Canada, in 2024. The patient, a thirteen-year-old, suffered severe respiratory failure but recovered with intensive care. The study analyzed viral mutations in this case and found unexpected results regarding receptor binding and cell fusion.
Scale, impact and risk
This case represents a single human infection with H5N1 avian flu. The study found that the mutations in hemagglutinin did not support binding to human-type receptors, and binding to avian receptors was severely diminished. However, the virus still replicated in human tissue, indicating potential for zoonotic transmission.
Response, status and uncertainty
The case was confirmed by public health agencies. The study was conducted by a lab, and the findings are based on virological tests, including sequencing and receptor binding analysis. The results suggest the need for further research to understand the mechanisms of zoonotic transmission and the implications for future outbreaks.

宿主 适应禽流感遵循“明确的季节性模式” - 饲料

Host-adapted bird flu follows ‘clear seasonal pattern’ - Feedstuffs
新闻简报The article published on October 6, 2026, by Feedstuffs highlights that host-adapted bird flu, specifically the H5N1 strain, follows a clear seasonal pattern linked to waterfowl migration. This pattern, observed in the Mississippi Flyway, has led to increased disease risk for poultry growers despite enhanced biosecurity measures. The outbreak, which began in 2022, has affected over 212 million birds and more than 1,000 commercial flocks since February 2022. Current cases are concentrated in the northern Plains states, and the virus is described as persisting in wild bird populations, allowing for seasonal spread. Researchers emphasize that understanding this pattern enables producers to prepare for peak exposure periods, but the recurring nature of the outbreak means the risk remains for the foreseeable future. The article does not provide specific confirmed cases or deaths but underscores the importance of community-level preparedness to mitigate the impact of the disease.
查看新闻五要素
时间
该文章发表于 2026 年 10 月 6 日。没有明确说明事件日期,但 HPAI 的季节性模式被描述为从 10 月到 5 月发生,夏季检测到稀疏。
地点与对象
暴发 集中在北部平原各州,密西西比迁飞路线被认为是家禽养殖者面临更高疾病风险的地区。受影响人群包括家禽养殖场和野生鸟类人群。
事件
文章报道,适应 宿主 的禽流感 (H5N1) 遵循明显的季节性模式,通过迁徙而不是逐户 传播 传播。这是根据贾达·汤普森和马修·马钱特的研究报告的观察结果。
规模、影响与风险
Between February 2022 and April 2026, HPAI affected 212 million birds, with over 1,000 commercial flocks impacted. The current host-adapted H5N1 virus is described as posing recurring seasonal risk for the foreseeable future.
应对、状态与不确定性
本文讨论了社区做好准备和提前规划以管理季节性风险的必要性。它还指出,已经采取了生物安全措施,但暴发继续通过外部引入传播。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Host-adapted bird flu follows ‘clear seasonal pattern’ - Feedstuffs

News Brief
The article published on October 6, 2026, by Feedstuffs highlights that host-adapted bird flu, specifically the H5N1 strain, follows a clear seasonal pattern linked to waterfowl migration. This pattern, observed in the Mississippi Flyway, has led to increased disease risk for poultry growers despite enhanced biosecurity measures. The outbreak, which began in 2022, has affected over 212 million birds and more than 1,000 commercial flocks since February 2022. Current cases are concentrated in the northern Plains states, and the virus is described as persisting in wild bird populations, allowing for seasonal spread. Researchers emphasize that understanding this pattern enables producers to prepare for peak exposure periods, but the recurring nature of the outbreak means the risk remains for the foreseeable future. The article does not provide specific confirmed cases or deaths but underscores the importance of community-level preparedness to mitigate the impact of the disease.

Number 8860726. keyboard_arrow_up QuotesWeatherDigital EditionsPodcasts menuMenusearchSearchsearchSearch menu Subscribe Subscribe Registeraccount_circleLog In search close menu Agribusiness Newskeyboard_arrow_down Related Topics Policy Commentary See Allchevron_right Market Newskeyboard_arrow_down Related Topics Grain Market News Livestock & Poultry Market News Market Reports See Allchevron_right Ingredient Market Priceskeyboard_arrow_down Related Topics Corn Price Updates Soybean Meal Price Updates See Allchevron_right Nutrition & Healthkeyboard_arrow_down Related Topics Poultry Health & Nutrition Beef Health & Nutrition Dairy Health & Nutrition Swine Health & Nutrition See Allchevron_right Feedstuffs 365 search close Poultry Health and Nutrition Nutrition & Health Research Host-adapted bird flu follows ‘clear seasonal pattern’ Understanding migration timing can help poultry growers plan ahead to protect their flocks from exposure. University of Arkansas System Division of Agriculture October 6, 2026 schedule2 Min Read University of Arkansas System Division of Agriculture/Fred Miller By Mary Hightower Understanding the timing of outbreaks of highly pathogenic avian influenza (HPAI) can help poultry farms prepare and protect their flocks, according to economists. Between February 2022 and April 2026, HPAI has affected 212 million birds, with more than 1,000 commercial flocks affected, according to the Animal & Plant Health Inspection Service, part of the U.S. Department of Agriculture. Jada Thompson, associate professor of agricultural economics for the University of Arkansas Division of Agriculture (UADA) who specializes in poultry, has done extensive research on the effects of HPAI. “When the United States poultry industry encountered the H5N2 strain of HPAI in 2014, it was mostly spread house-to-house through contaminated materials, trucks and laborers,” she said. “This prompted sweeping changes in biosecurity culture that still exist today,” Thompson said. “These changes, along with a hot summer, helped to end the outbreak by June 2015. Related:How nest cover and light affect egg laying in cage-free systems “However, since the 2022 introduction of a host-adapted H5N1 virus, poultry growers in the Mississippi Flyway have faced increased disease risk, despite the wide adoption of these enhanced biosecurity measures. This most recent outbreak has spread instead by external introduction rather than house to house as in 2014,” she said. “‘Host-adapted’ refers to the virus’s ability to persist in wild bird populations. Where previous HPAI events ravaged both poultry and wild birds, the current H5N1 2.3.4.4b classification is less likely to kill some wild bird species,” Thompson said. “This means that waterfowl can become infected and still survive migration, spreading the disease across the flyway.” “Examining the geographic distribution of HPAI detections over time reveals a clear seasonal pattern that mirrors waterfowl migration,” said Matthew Marchant, whose findings are contained in the paper “Bird Flu on the Move,” published in Southern Ag Today earlier this year. Marchant is a Ph.D. student in environmental dynamics at the University of Arkansas, working with Thompson in the department of agricultural economics and agribusiness. Thompson is a faculty member with UADA’s research arm, the Arkansas Agricultural Experiment Station, and the Dale Bumpers College of Agricultural, Food and Life Sciences at the University of Arkansas. “During the summer months, detections are sparse and concentrated in the northern reaches of the flyway, where birds spend the breeding season. As fall migration begins, HPAI’s geographic footprint expands southward, spanning nearly the entire flyway from October through May,” he said. Related:Chore-Time introduces CATALYST OVO for layer operations “Understanding seasonality affords producers a predictable window each year to prepare before peak exposure periods arrive. A host-adapted virus in a migratory population means recurring seasonal risk for the foreseeable future. However, that seasonality also gives producers, researchers and communities time to plan ahead,” Marchant said. “Understanding when and where the disease is likely to appear turns an unpredictable force into a manageable calendar of preparation and vigilance,” Marchant said. “Community preparedness, which begins at the farm level, remains the most effective tool available against this disease.” According to APHIS, current cases are concentrated in the northern Plains states. The article was published on October 6, 2026. The event date is not explicitly stated, but the seasonal pattern of HPAI is described as occurring from October through May, with sparse detections during the summer months.
Location and population
The outbreak is concentrated in the northern Plains states, and the Mississippi Flyway is mentioned as a region where poultry growers have faced increased disease risk. The affected population includes poultry farms and wild bird populations.
Event
The article reports that host-adapted bird flu (H5N1) follows a clear seasonal pattern, spreading through migration rather than house-to-house transmission. This is a reported observation based on research by Jada Thompson and Matthew Marchant.
Scale, impact and risk

马尼托巴省再次检测到 禽流感 - WATTPoultry.com

Avian influenza detected again in Manitoba - WATTPoultry.com
新闻简报加拿大马尼托巴省再次检测到 高致病性禽流感 (HPAI),最新确诊病例于 2026 年 10 月 2 日在彭比纳 (Pembina) 发现。这标志着彭比纳 (Pembina) 2026 年首次出现 HPAI 病例,此前从 2025 年 12 月至 2026 年 9 月期间没有报告病例。在过去的一个月里,感染 HPAI 的加拿大家禽群中有十分之八在曼尼托巴省,其余 2 只在艾伯塔省。加拿大食品检验局 (CFIA) 继续监测 暴发,并提供了交互式地图来跟踪北美各地的 HPAI 病例。然而,加拿大食品检验局尚未透露有关受影响鸡群的具体细节,包括其规模或涉及的家禽类型。暴发 强调了 HPAI 对商业家禽的持续威胁,CFIA 强调需要持续进行 监测 和监测。
查看新闻五要素
时间
最新病例于 2026 年 10 月 2 日确诊。2026 年 9 月的早期病例包括莱茵兰的肉火鸡以及卡地亚和罗克伍德的混合鸡群。过去一个月,暴发 一直在曼尼托巴省进行。
地点与对象
马尼托巴省,特别是彭比纳市,以及加拿大其他地区,如莱茵兰、卡地亚和罗克伍德。受影响的种群包括商业家禽群,主要是火鸡以及肉鸡、蛋鸡和火鸡的混合群。
事件
马尼托巴省的商业家禽中再次检测到 高致病性禽流感 (HPAI)。最新确诊病例于 2026 年 10 月 2 日发生在彭比纳。这是彭比纳 2026 年首例病例,曼尼托巴省在 2025 年 12 月至 2026 年 9 月期间没有出现 HPAI 病例。
规模、影响与风险
过去一个月,感染高致病性禽流感的加拿大 10 只家禽中有 8 只在马尼托巴省,其余 2 只在艾伯塔省。CFIA 继续监测局势,并提供了交互式地图来跟踪北美各地的高致病性禽流感病例。该风险被明确表述为持续存在的威胁。
应对、状态与不确定性
加拿大食品检验局(CFIA)正在监测事态发展,尚未透露有关受影响鸡群的更多细节。CFIA 传统上不会发布有关受影响经营场所的鸡群规模或家禽类型的信息。可以获取先前报告案件的最新情况,但有关彭比纳案件的具体细节尚未披露。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Avian influenza detected again in Manitoba - WATTPoultry.com

News Brief
Highly pathogenic avian influenza (HPAI) has been detected again in Manitoba, Canada, with the latest confirmed case in Pembina on October 2, 2026. This marks the first HPAI case in Pembina during 2026, following a period of no reported cases from December 2025 through September 2026. During the past month, eight out of ten Canadian poultry flocks infected by HPAI were in Manitoba, with the remaining two in Alberta. The Canadian Food Inspection Agency (CFIA) continues to monitor the outbreak and has provided an interactive map for tracking HPAI cases across North America. However, the CFIA has not disclosed specific details about the affected flock, including its size or the type of poultry involved. The outbreak highlights the ongoing threat of HPAI in commercial poultry, with the CFIA emphasizing the need for continued surveillance and monitoring.

Roy Graber October 5, 2026 Tatiana53 | Bigstock Article Summary Highly pathogenic avian influenza has been detected again in Manitoba's commercial poultry, with the latest confirmed case in Pembina on October 2, 2026. Eight of the ten Canadian poultry flocks infected by HPAI in the past month were in Manitoba, with the remaining two in Alberta. Latest case confirmed October 2, 2026 in Pembina municipality, Manitoba Eight of ten commercial poultry flocks affected by HPAI in Canada were in Manitoba First Pembina case in 2026; Manitoba had no HPAI cases from December 2025 through September 2026 Earlier September cases included meat turkeys in Rhineland and mixed flocks of broilers, laying hens, and turkeys in Cartier and Rockwood CFIA continues monitoring; interactive map available for tracking HPAI cases across North America Highly pathogenic avian influenza (HPAI) continues to be found in commercial poultry in Manitoba. The Canadian Food Inspection Agency (CFIA) reported that the latest case in Manitoba was confirmed Oct. 2 in the municipality of Pembina. Further information about that flock infection has not yet been disclosed by CFIA. During the past month, 10 commercial poultry flocks in Canada have been struck by HPAI. Of those, eight have been in Manitoba, while the remaining two were in Alberta. This is the first case in Pembina during 2026. Until September, Manitoba had not had any instances of HPAI in poultry since December 2025. Updates on previously reported cases CFIA has traditionally not released information concerning flock sizes or the type of poultry in operations that have been affected by HPAI. However, a recent report from the World Organisation for Animal Health (WOAH) offered additional details on earlier reported cases that were all confirmed on Sept. 21 in Manitoba. An affected flock in Rhineland included meat turkeys, while a flock struck in Cartier was a mixed flock of broilers and laying hens. A third flock, in Rockwood, was also a mixed flock, but in this situation, the flock included broilers, laying hens and turkeys. To learn more about HPAI cases in commercial poultry flocks in the United States, Mexico and Canada, see an interactive map on WATTPoultry.com. Manitoba, specifically the Pembina municipality, and other regions in Canada such as Rhineland, Cartier, and Rockwood. The affected population includes commercial poultry flocks, primarily turkeys and mixed flocks of broilers, laying hens, and turkeys.
Event
Highly pathogenic avian influenza (HPAI) has been detected again in Manitoba's commercial poultry. The latest confirmed case occurred in Pembina on October 2, 2026. This is the first case in Pembina during 2026, and Manitoba had no HPAI cases from December 2025 through September 2026.
Scale, impact and risk
During the past month, eight of the ten Canadian poultry flocks infected by HPAI were in Manitoba, with the remaining two in Alberta. The CFIA continues monitoring the situation and has provided an interactive map for tracking HPAI cases across North America. The risk is explicitly stated as ongoing and a continued threat.
Response, status and uncertainty
The Canadian Food Inspection Agency (CFIA) is monitoring the situation and has not disclosed further details about the affected flock. The CFIA traditionally does not release information on flock sizes or poultry types in affected operations. Updates on previously reported cases are available, but specific details about the Pembina case have not been disclosed.

Vaxart 宣布发表临床前研究,证明在雪貂挑战模型中对致命剂量的 H5N1 禽流感具有 100% 的保护作用

Vaxart Announces Publication of Preclinical Study Demonstrating 100% Protection Against a Lethal Dose of H5N1 Avian Flu in a Ferret Challenge Model
新闻简报Vaxart Inc.于2026年10月8日宣布,发表的临床前研究结果显示,其第二代口腔粘膜疫苗候选药物VXA-H5-02在雪貂攻击模型中针对致命剂量的H5N1禽流感提供100%的保护。这项发表在《免疫学前沿》上的研究表明,接种疫苗的雪貂在 H5N1 进化枝 2.3.4.4b 的挑战中幸存下来,而未接种疫苗的对照在第 6 天时 死亡率 达到 100%。疫苗 在第 8-9 天显着减少了病毒脱落并清除了病毒 RNA。这些发现凸显了 Vaxart 的口服 疫苗 平台在预防流感(特别是 H5N1)方面的潜力,但尚未报告人类病例、死亡或暴露情况。结果基于同行评审的临床前研究,被认为是初步的,没有提及现实世界的应用或结果。

该研究于2026年10月8日发表,临床前结果此前于2025年报告。

该研究使用雪貂作为挑战模型进行,没有提及具体的地理位置或人口。

已发表的临床前研究结果表明,在雪貂攻击模型中,对致命剂量的 H5N1 禽流感具有 100% 的保护作用。该研究由 Vaxart 进行,并由 GlobeNewswire 报道。
规模、影响与风险
该研究显示,接种疫苗的雪貂在接受 H5N1 clade 2.3.4.4b 病毒攻击后,存活率为 100%,而未接种疫苗的对照组在第 6 天的 死亡率 存活率为 100%。结果表明,雪貂具有强大的免疫保护并减少了病毒脱落,但没有人类病例、死亡或暴露的报告。
应对、状态与不确定性
该研究处于临床前阶段,已发表在《免疫学前沿》上。它证明了对雪貂的保护功效,但没有人体试验或现实世界结果的报道。该研究结果基于一项同行评审的研究,被认为是初步的。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Vaxart Announces Publication of Preclinical Study Demonstrating 100% Protection Against a Lethal Dose of H5N1 Avian Flu in a Ferret Challenge Model

News Brief
Vaxart Inc. announced on October 8, 2026, the publication of preclinical study results showing that its second-generation oral mucosal vaccine candidate, VXA-H5-02, provides 100% protection against a lethal dose of H5N1 avian flu in a ferret challenge model. The study, published in Frontiers in Immunology, demonstrated that vaccinated ferrets survived a challenge with H5N1 clade 2.3.4.4b, while unvaccinated controls had 100% mortality by Day 6. The vaccine significantly reduced viral shedding and cleared viral RNA by Day 8–9. These findings highlight the potential of Vaxart’s oral vaccine platform for influenza prevention, particularly for H5N1, but no human cases, deaths, or exposures are reported. The results are based on a peer-reviewed preclinical study and are considered preliminary, with no mention of real-world application or outcomes.
Original source text (English)
My Markets Watchlist Markets News Search Business Strategy Economy Finance Retail Advertising Careers Law Media Real Estate Small Business The Better Work Project Personal Finance Tech Science AI Enterprise Transportation Startups Innovation Markets News Stocks Indices Commodities Crypto Currencies ETFs Lifestyle Entertainment Culture Travel Food Health Education Parenting Military & Defense Politics Reviews Home Kitchen Style Streaming Pets Tech Deals Gifts Tickets Video Big Business So Expensive View From Above Small Business Authorized Account Risky Business Boot Camp Still Standing How Crime Works Life Lessons Level Up Community Games Boxed Pipeline Knighthop Tally Vaxart Announces Publication of Preclinical Study Demonstrating 100% Protection Against a Lethal Dose of H5N1 Avian Flu in a Ferret Challenge Model PRESS RELEASE GlobeNewswire Oct. 8, 2026, 08:40 AM Second-generation oral mucosal vaccine candidate (VXA-H5-02) induced robust cross-neutralizing antibodies, significantly reduced viral load, and completely protected against lethal clade 2.3.4.4b viral challenge SOUTH SAN FRANCISCO, Calif., Oct. 08, 2026 (GLOBE NEWSWIRE) -- Vaxart, Inc. (OTCQX: VXRT), a clinical-stage biotechnology company developing oral recombinant vaccines administered via tablet, today announced the publication of positive preclinical study results evaluating its adenovirus-based oral mucosal vaccine platform against Highly Pathogenic Avian Influenza (HPAI) H5N1, a pathogenic virus that poses a significant threat to public health. The complete, peer-reviewed study results, which were published in Frontiers in Immunology, demonstrate that Vaxart’s second-generation vaccine candidate, VXA-H5-02, elicits both mucosal and systemic neutralizing antibody responses, offering comprehensive protection against lethal challenge with H5N1 clade 2.3.4.4b. The company previously reported topline study results in 2025. "The potential spread of H5N1 avian influenza across livestock and mammals underlines the critical need for scalable, rapidly deployable vaccines," said Dr. Sean Tucker, CSO of Vaxart. "These data further support our oral mucosal vaccination technology and highlight the improved immune-stimulating activity of our second-generation H5 construct. The 100% survival rate following challenge with a lethal dose of virus underscores the robust immune protection our oral influenza vaccine candidates may be able to provide. This protection, combined with ease of self-administration and the elimination of complex cold-chain requirements required for injected vaccines, supports the potential of our platform in enabling a novel approach to influenza prevention and pandemic preparedness.” Key Study Highlights 100% Survival Rate: All ferrets immunized with VXA-H5-02 survived a lethal challenge with H5N1 clade 2.3.4.4b, whereas 100% of unvaccinated control animals rapidly succumbed to infection by Day 6 post-challenge (p< 0.001). Significant Viral Load & Shedding Reduction: VXA-H5-02 vaccination produced a ~2.3-log reduction in infectious viral shedding by Day 3 post-challenge compared to controls. By Day 6–7, viable virus was below the limit of detection in nasal wash samples, and 7 out of 8 animals completely cleared viral RNA by Day 8/9. Mucosal & Cross-Neutralizing Immunity: Immunization with VXA-H5-02 elicited strong serum neutralizing titers and mucosal IgA/IgG responses in nasal wash fluid. Higher pre-challenge nasal IgA levels showed the strongest negative correlation with post-challenge viral shedding (r = -0.6059). Cross-Clade Protection: Serum from VXA-H5-02 immunized animals demonstrated cross-neutralizing activity against divergent H5 strains, including the recently emerged H5N1 clade 2.3.2.1e. Additionally, the clade-mismatched first-generation vaccine (ND1.1, encoding clade 2.1.3.2) conferred 37% survival against the clade 2.3.4.4b challenge. About the Study In the study, non-replicating recombinant adenovirus type 5 (rAd5) vectors expressing viral hemagglutinin (HA) were administered directly to the small intestine of ferrets via endoscopic delivery on Day 0 and Day 28. Ferrets cannot use human tablets, so a liquid formulation delivery method was used to simulate enteric-coated tablet release. On Day 56, animals were challenged intranasally with 1 x 105 TCID50 H5N1 clade 2.3.4.4b virus. Protective efficacy, clinical symptoms, mucosal/systemic antibody titers, and viral shedding (measured by RT-qPCR and TCID50) were systematically monitored. About Vaxart Vaxart is a clinical-stage biotechnology company developing a range of oral recombinant vaccines based on its proprietary delivery platform. Vaxart vaccines are designed to be administered using pills that can be stored and shipped without refrigeration and eliminate the risk of needle-stick injury. Vaxart believes that its proprietary pill vaccine delivery platform is suitable to deliver recombinant vaccines, positioning the Company to develop oral versions of currently marketed vaccines and to design recombinant vaccines for new indications. Vaxart’s development programs currently include pill vaccines designed to protect against coronavirus, norovirus, and influenza, as well as a therapeutic vaccine for human papillomavirus (HPV), Vaxart’s first immune-oncology indication. Vaxart has filed broad domestic and international patent applications covering its proprietary technology and creations for oral vaccination using adenovirus and TLR3 agonists. 禽流感 暴发 欧洲和英国评估更新 - IndexBox

Avian Influenza Outbreak Assessments Updated for Europe and UK - IndexBox
新闻简报英国政府更新了对英国和欧洲 高致病性禽流感 (HPAI) 的 暴发 评估,最新更新于 2026 年 10 月 5 日。这些评估重点关注与鸟类聚集相关的 HPAI H5Nx 传播,例如集市、表演、市场和销售。之前的评估是在 2026 年 8 月、2026 年 6 月和前几个月添加的,强调了持续的监测工作。该出版物指出,风险评估会定期发布,早期报告可在国家档案馆网站上找到。这些评估被描述为初步评估和更新评估,表明情况正在调查中。没有报告具体病例或死亡人数,但正在评估传播风险,作为更广泛的疾病控制措施的一部分。政府还提供了识别和预防 禽流感 的指导,强调监测和报告新 暴发 的重要性。
查看新闻五要素
时间
欧洲和英国 HPAI 的更新 暴发 评估于 2026 年 10 月 5 日添加,之前的评估涵盖 2026 年 2 月至 2025 年 12 月的多个月份。发布日期为 2026 年 10 月 5 日。
地点与对象
评估覆盖英国和欧洲,包括荷兰,荷兰于 2026 年 3 月 3 日增加了对奶牛 HPAI 抗体的初步评估。
事件
英国政府的出版物包括对英国和欧洲 高致病性禽流感 (HPAI) 的最新 暴发 评估,特别提到了与鸟类聚集相关的 HPAI H5Nx。这些评估被描述为基于政府记录的初步评估和更新评估。
规模、影响与风险
评估重点关注与鸟类博览会、表演、市场、销售和其他聚会相关的 HPAI H5Nx 传播的可能性。提供的证据中没有报告具体病例或死亡人数,但风险评估是持续监测工作的一部分。
应对、状态与不确定性
这些评估是定期更新和风险审查的一部分,表明 暴发 状态正在接受调查,政府正在监测 HPAI H5Nx 的传播。该出版物指出,国家档案馆网站上提供了早期报告,并提供了识别和预防该疾病的指导。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Avian Influenza Outbreak Assessments Updated for Europe and UK - IndexBox

News Brief
The UK government has updated outbreak assessments for highly pathogenic avian influenza (HPAI) in Great Britain and Europe, with the most recent addition on 5 October 2026. These assessments focus on the spread of HPAI H5Nx associated with bird gatherings, such as fairs, shows, markets, and sales. Previous assessments were added in August 2026, June 2026, and earlier months, highlighting ongoing monitoring efforts. The publication notes that risk assessments are periodically issued, and earlier reports are available on the National Archives website. The assessments are described as preliminary and updated, indicating that the situation is under investigation. No specific case or death counts are reported, but the risk of spread is being evaluated as part of broader disease control measures. The government also provides guidance on identifying and preventing avian influenza, emphasizing the importance of monitoring and reporting new outbreaks.

The source, the UK government's avian influenza in Europe publication, shows a running series of preliminary and updated assessments covering high pathogenicity avian influenza (HPAI) in Great Britain and Europe. The most recent addition came on 5 October 2026, when an updated outbreak assessment for HPAI in Great Britain and Europe for September 2026 was added. Before that, on 18 August 2026, an updated assessment for August 2026 was added. Risk assessments focused on the likelihood of spread of highly pathogenic avian influenza H5Nx associated with bird fairs, shows, markets, sales and other gatherings have also been issued periodically. A June 2026 risk assessment on that topic was added on 23 June 2026, following an April 2026 version added on 22 April 2026 and a March 2026 version added on 25 March 2026. A February 2026 version was added on 6 March 2026. Outbreak assessments for Great Britain and Europe were also added on 1 June 2026, covering 26 May 2026, and on 15 April 2026, covering 10 April 2026. Further assessments were added on 20 March 2026, covering 10 March 2026, and on 10 March 2026, covering 27 February 2026. On 3 March 2026, a preliminary outbreak assessment dated 13 February 2026 was added concerning HPAI antibodies in dairy cattle in the Netherlands. On 4 February 2026, a qualitative risk assessment on HPAI H5 and the control of wild birds under general licences GL40 to GL42 for the period from January 2024 to December 2025 was added; that report was written in December 2024. Earlier additions include an updated outbreak assessment for 19 December 2025, added on 24 December 2025, and a risk assessment on the likelihood of spread of HPAI H5Nx associated with bird fairs, shows, markets, sales and other gatherings, added on 8 December 2025. An updated assessment for 11 November 2025 was added on 17 November 2025, and one for 28 October 2025 was added on 3 November 2025. An updated assessment for 8 October 2025 was added on 21 October 2025. On 16 September 2025, a risk assessment on the likelihood of spread of HPAI H5Nx associated with bird fairs, shows, markets, sales and other gatherings was attached following a change in national risk levels for HPAI. An updated assessment for 11 August 2025 was added on 15 August 2025, and a risk assessment on the same gatherings topic was added on 7 August 2025. Risk levels in the updated outbreak assessment for 21 July 2025 were corrected on 30 July 2025, and that assessment was added on 29 July 2025. A risk assessment on the likelihood of spread of HPAI H5Nx associated with bird fairs, shows, markets, sales and other gatherings for May 2025 was added on 20 June 2025. The 12 May 2025 outbreak assessment was added on 13 May 2025, and the 18 March 2025 assessment was added on 24 March 2025. A risk assessment on the same gatherings topic for January 2025 was added on 20 February 2025. The record also lists assessments and reports from earlier years, including outbreak assessments for Great Britain and Europe, risk assessments on gatherings and on the incursion of HPAI into housed or not housed poultry flocks and captive birds, and scientific opinions. The publication notes that major, notifiable or new and emerging animal disease outbreaks worldwide are monitored, and that a preliminary outbreak assessment may be carried out when a new animal disease outbreak becomes known in the UK or another country. It also states that earlier reports are available on the National Archives website and points to detailed guidance on spotting the disease, what to do if it is suspected, and measures to prevent it. The updated outbreak assessments for HPAI in Europe and the UK were added on 5 October 2026, with previous assessments covering various months from February 2026 to December 2025. The publication date is 5 October 2026.
Location and population
The assessments cover Great Britain and Europe, including the Netherlands where a preliminary assessment on HPAI antibodies in dairy cattle was added on 3 March 2026.
Event
The UK government's publication includes updated outbreak assessments for highly pathogenic avian influenza (HPAI) in Great Britain and Europe, with a specific mention of HPAI H5Nx associated with bird gatherings. These assessments are described as preliminary and updated, based on government records.
Scale, impact and risk
The assessments focus on the likelihood of spread of HPAI H5Nx linked to bird fairs, shows, markets, sales, and other gatherings. No specific case or death counts are reported in the evidence provided, but the risk assessments are part of ongoing monitoring efforts.
Response, status and uncertainty
The assessments are part of periodic updates and risk reviews, indicating that the outbreak status is under investigation and that the government is monitoring the spread of HPAI H5Nx. The publication notes that earlier reports are available on the National Archives website and provides guidance on identifying and preventing the disease.

亚基马县奶牛场发现禽流感,华盛顿首例牛病例 - 不间断本地新闻

Bird Flu found in Yakima County dairy farms, first cattle cases in Washington - Nonstop Local News
新闻简报2026 年 10 月 8 日,亚基马县奶牛场发现禽流感,这是华盛顿州首例牛病例。本顿·富兰克林卫生区报告了该州首例与禽流感有关的死亡病例。这一进展凸显了 禽流感 A 病毒向牲畜传播的潜在可能性,引发了对公共卫生和人畜共患 传播 的担忧。目前情况正在调查中,卫生区正在监测其影响。来源是联合摘要,无法提取完整的原始文章正文。这一事件强调了在追踪禽源人畜共患感染及其对动物和人类健康的影响时保持警惕的必要性。
查看新闻五要素
时间
2026 年 10 月 8 日@ 下午 6:13。
地点与对象
华盛顿州亚基马县的奶牛场。
事件
亚基马县奶牛场发现禽流感,这是华盛顿州首例牛病例。本顿富兰克林卫生区报告了华盛顿首例与禽流感相关的死亡病例。
规模、影响与风险
据报道有 1 人死亡。该事件与高致病性的 禽流感 A 病毒有关。风险没有明确说明,但牛体内存在禽流感表明存在潜在的公共卫生问题。
应对、状态与不确定性
本顿富兰克林卫生区参与报告和监测情况。来源是联合摘要,无法提取完整的原始文章正文。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Bird Flu found in Yakima County dairy farms, first cattle cases in Washington - Nonstop Local News

News Brief
On October 8, 2026, bird flu was detected in Yakima County dairy farms, marking the first cattle cases in Washington. The Benton Franklin Health District reported the first death linked to bird flu in the state. This development highlights the potential spread of avian influenza A viruses to livestock, raising concerns about public health and zoonotic transmission. The situation is under investigation, and the health district is monitoring the impact. The source is a syndicated summary, and the full original article body could not be extracted. This event underscores the need for vigilance in tracking avian-origin zoonotic infections and their implications for both animal and human health.

Original source text (Chinese)
韩国野生鸟类中新型重配高致病性禽流感A(H5N1) clade 2.3.4.4b病毒的多重引入、基因型多样性及贝叶斯系统发育动力学(2024–2025) 《Veterinary Research》:Multiple introductions and spread of novel reassortant highly pathogenic avian influenza A (H5N1) clade 2.3.4.4b viruses via wild birds, South Korea, 2024–2025 【字体: 大 中 小 】 时间:2026年10月09日 来源:Veterinary Research 3.8 编辑推荐: 2024年10月14日至2025年3月24日期间,通过国家高致病性禽流感(highly pathogenic avian influenza,HPAI)监测项目,在野生鸟类中检测到43例clade 2.3.4.4b HPAI A型(H5N1)感染,并在1只豹猫 欢迎领域“高通量小分子筛选解决方案”>> 为了促进科学的传播,生物通采集了这篇文章 订阅BioHot点这里解锁原文链接推送! “畅读会员”点此登录查看原文链接 Research Open access 2024年10月14日至2025年3月24日期间,通过国家高致病性禽流感(highly pathogenic avian influenza,HPAI)监测项目,在野生鸟类中检测到43例clade 2.3.4.4b HPAI A型(H5N1)感染,并在1只豹猫中检测到1例。研究人员对51株分离株进行了全基因组测序,并调查了其系统发育关系。系统发育分析鉴定出9种不同基因型,命名为Kor24–25A至Kor24–25I。最常检出的基因型Kor24–25A(n = 24)在鹈形目(Pelecaniformes)中频繁检出,并在2024年12月至2025年1月间最为流行。使用HA(hemagglutinin,血凝素)基因序列进行的贝叶斯连续和离散性状系统发育动力学分析(Bayesian continuous and discrete trait phylodynamic analyses)确定了经由京畿道、济州岛和朝鲜半岛南部沿海地区的3条最可能病毒引入途径,并提示野鸭是促进病毒在野生鸟类种群中传播的主要储存宿主。2024–2025季节期间,大白鹭(Ardea alba)似乎比其他野生鸟种更频繁受累,提示野生鸟类中物种特异性疾病结局可能存在季节性差异。该研究为HPAI H5N1病毒的系统发育动力学、宿主关联和传播模式提供了重要见解。它强调针对关键禽类宿主和高风险地理区域的强化监测项目的重要性。 论文解读:韩国野生鸟类中新型重配HPAI H5N1 clade 2.3.4.4b病毒的多重引入与传播(2024–2025) 欢迎领取《放射性同位素检测一站式解决方案》 领 取 研究背景:A/Goose/Guangdong/1/1996(Gs/Gd)谱系H5高致病性禽流感(HPAI)病毒1996年在中国家鹅中首次发现,之后在家禽和野鸟中反复暴发。其血凝素(HA)基因已分化为0–9 clades及多个亚支。clade 2.3.4于2008年在中国出现,演化为2.3.4.4a–h。clade 2.3.4.4b自2014年起与当地禽流感病毒广泛重配,产生H5N2、H5N5、H5N6、H5N8等。至2021年末,clade 2.3.4.4b H5N1成为全球优势HPAI亚型,在亚洲、欧洲、非洲和北美野鸟和家禽中广泛暴发,并因宿主范围扩大和反复溢出至哺乳动物(包括人)引发公共卫生关注。韩国自2003年首次HPAI H5N1侵入后,反复发生H5Nx(H5N1、H5N8、H5N6)暴发,野鸟在引入和传播中持续起关键作用。2020–2021冬季报告234例野鸟clade 2.3.4.4b HPAI病例,2021–2022为47起,2022–2023为174例,2023–2024为19例,季节间规模和病毒活动差异明显,并出现不同基因型,提示反复引入和野鸟种群内重配。2024–2025季节clade 2.3.4.4b H5N1经野鸟传入韩国,且2025年3月在野生豹猫(Prionailurus bengalensis)中检测到HPAI H5N1,为韩国该野生哺乳动物首次记录的溢出事件,显示宿主范围扩大和对动物及公共卫生的持续威胁。因此,研究人员对2024–2025冬季韩国野鸟和豹猫H5N1病毒进行全基因组测序和比较系统发育分析,以阐明时空扩散路径、进化动态和宿主转换。 研究人员开展:2024年10月14日至2025年3月24日,通过国家HPAI监测项目在野生鸟类中报告43例clade 2.3.4.4b HPAI H5N1感染,1例豹猫。研究人员对51株H5N1分离株进行全基因组测序,并开展系统发育和贝叶斯系统发育动力学分析。结论:鉴定出9种基因型Kor24–25A至Kor24–25I;Kor24–25A(n=24)最常见,在鹈形目频繁检出,2024年12月至2025年1月最流行;贝叶斯分析基于HA识别三条最可能引入路线,经京畿、济州和朝鲜半岛南部沿海;野鸭为主要储存宿主;大白鹭更频繁受累,提示季节性物种差异。意义:提供HPAI H5N1系统发育动力学、宿主关联和传播模式见解,强调针对关键禽类宿主和高风险地区加强监测。 关键技术方法(不超过250字):样本来源为2024年10月至2025年3月由韩国国家野生动物疾病控制与预防研究所(NIWDC)开展的全国野动物监测,覆盖92个监测点,收集57,067份粪便和1,007份个体鸟拭子,并被动检查419具尸体;从43起暴发中获得51株病毒,包括2025年3月16日豹猫分离株。关键技术包括:实时逆转录聚合酶链反应(rRT-PCR)筛查M和H5基因;Illumina MiSeq全基因组测序并提交GISAID;MAFFT比对,RAxML最大似然(ML)系统发育,iTOL可视化,PopArt最小生成网络,ggFLU基因型分类,FluMut分子标记筛查;TempEst评估分子钟信号;BEAST/BEAUti贝叶斯连续和离散性状系统发育动力学分析,CIPRES运行,Tracer、LogCombiner、TreeAnnotator、FigTree、EvoLaps、SpreaD3和PACT分析传播路线、宿主转换和状态比例。 研究结果: **Multiple genotypes of 2024–2025 HPAI viruses**:研究人员成功对2024年10月14日至2025年3月24日韩国野生动物中51株H5N1 HPAI病毒测序。ML系统发育显示所有基因片段存在多个遗传簇,bootstrap支持>90:PB2三个、PB1四个、PA三个、HA两个、NP五个、NA两个、M一个、NS一个。多数簇内序列>98%成对核苷酸同一性,但A/Mandarin duck/Korea/24WF743–8P/2025的PB1、A/Wild goose/Korea/24WF894–1P/2025和A/Mandarin duck/Korea/24WF266–5P/2024的NS例外。鉴定九种基因型:Kor24–25A(AAAAAAAA;n=24)、Kor24–25B(BBBBBBAB;n=11)、Kor24–25C(AAABAAAA;n=8)、Kor24–25D(AAAADAAA;n=1)、Kor24–25E(ACAACAAA;n=2)、Kor24–25F(CCCACAAA;n=2)、Kor24–25G(AAAAEAAA;n=1)、Kor24–25H(ADAAAAAA;n=1)、Kor24–25I(DAAAAAAA;n=1)。最小生成网络显示病毒大体按基因型聚类,但核苷酸替换数不总严格对应基因型边界。Kor24–25A、B、C为主要基因型;C的HA与B近,其余七片段与A近;其他六种为短暂次要变异,各1–2株,多数片段与A共享但内部基因不同。Kor24–25D、G、H的PB1或NP来自东亚既往HPAI谱系;Kor24–25E、F、I的PB2或NP与中亚和东亚低致病性禽流感(LPAI)病毒近;E和F的PB1形成独立簇,bootstrap 100,与欧洲–俄罗斯HPAI病毒同一性97.5–98.0%。ggFLU等分类显示Kor24–25B对应B23.2–1,GenoFLU A3/A5,GenIn2 EA–2020–C;其余40样本ggFLU为B.18,GenIn2和GenoFLU未分配。FluMut发现十种突变组合A–J;Kor24–25A含F、E、C型,B含G、J型,C含H、I型,其他各单一型,F由四种基因型共享。 **Temporal and host distributions of HPAI viruses**:2024年10月至2025年3月,51株病毒周分布显示,2024年10月第三周首次检测时Kor24–25A和B同时出现,2024年11月D、E、C出现。野鸟HPAI病例2024年12月达峰。2024年12月第三周优势基因型从B转为A。A全季持续,B在2024年12月后未记录;次要基因型2024年12月后零星检出。宿主分布中,鹈形目占最高比例,其次雁形目、猛禽(Falconiformes和Accipitriformes)、鸻形目和食肉目。鹈形目中大白鹭最多(n=22),主要关联Kor24–25A。雁形目基因型多样性最高,检出7种。猛禽检出A、B、C、I四种。三种主要基因型跨多种宿主:A和B除食肉目外所有宿主组,C见于雁形目、食肉目和猛禽。 **Spatiotemporal transmission patterns of HPAI viruses**:基于HA的贝叶斯系统发育动力学揭示2024–2025冬季H5N1时空传播。cluster 1首例为2024年10月14日京畿地区捕获鸳鸯,HA来自Kor24–25A,tMRCA为2024年5月6日(95% HPD:2023年12月20日至2024年8月29日)。连续系统地理学提示京畿道为cluster 1在韩国最可能祖先地点,随后向南部扩散,部分再向北,另一些可能传播至日本南部。cluster 2含Kor24–25B和C的HA,分2a(主要B)和2b(主要C),提示独立引入。2a tMRCA为2024年7月7日(95% HPD:2024年3月27日至9月20日);2b为2024年7月27日(95% HPD:2024年3月23日至11月9日);cluster 2整体tMRCA为2024年2月23日(95% HPD:2023年11月11日至2024年7月26日)。2a最可能祖先地点为济州,随后向北并向东西扩散;2b最可能为朝鲜半岛南部沿海,分两路,一路向北至忠清,一路向南至济州,北上病毒后来沿西部沿海向南移动。 **Transmission of HPAI viruses between host species**:基于HA的宿主离散性状分析中,BF>3且PP>0.5为显著,BF>20且PP>0.9为强支持。cluster 1最可能经野鸭引入(BF=5.448,PP=0.708);随后野鸭至鹭(BF=30.831,PP=0.932)和其他野鸟(BF=5.467,PP=0.708);鹭至其他野鸟(BF=23.587,PP=0.913);野鸭至韩国外谱系(BF=43.286,PP=0.951)强支持。cluster 2同样可能经野鸭从韩国外引入(BF=75.497,PP=0.971);野鸭至其他野鸟(BF=89.554,PP=0.997)和鹭(BF=39.928,PP=0.947)强支持;未推断鹭起源传播。祖先离散状态主干比例随时间依次为韩国外、韩国野鸭、韩国鹭、韩国其他。源–汇动态显示两簇转换事件均发生在2024年Q3,从野鸭到鹭和其他物种;cluster 1另有2024年Q4和2025年Q1鹭到其他物种。Markov rewards显示cluster 1野鸭最高,其次鹭和其他;cluster 2野鸭最高,其他次之,鹭最低。 讨论部分总结:研究人员报告2024–2025冬季野鸟中九种新HPAI基因型,提示clade 2.3.4.4b HPAI与共循环HPAI或LPAI频繁重配。内部基因中M和NS遗传多样性最低,NP形成最多单系枝。Kor24–25A、B、C为主要基因型,时间分布不同;A持续整个监测期,B仅早期至2024年12月第五周,约占采样期一半。早期基因型短暂优势此前已有,但与2021–2022和2022–2023冬季随暴发进展基因型多样性下降不同,本季持续遗传多样性。A、B、C在多种野鸟检出,宿主范围广。雁形目基因型多样性最高,符合其主要水鸟储存库角色;鹭至其他野鸟传播提示鹭可作易感宿主和潜在中间宿主参与本地传播。猛禽基因型多样性第二,与既往低或无禽流感病毒流行报道不同,可能因捕食感染水禽。豹猫Kor24–25C零星感染可能因食入感染野鸟。HA贝叶斯分析识别三个病毒簇,表明至少三次独立引入。cluster 1引入地区既往为HPAI侵入高风险区,来自俄罗斯、蒙古、中国风险高于日本;cluster 2引入地区济州和南部与日本引入风险较高,且2024–2025日本H5 HPAI HA分析显示来自cluster 2a的24WC025与同期日本分离株同枝。2a和2b可能经野鸟独立引入不同地区,所有基因序列与既往野鸟病毒近,重配更可能发生在亚洲迁徙野鸟而非家禽。离散性状分析提示季节特异性宿主分布和传播。既往研究认为野鸭在早期引入和国内传播重要,鹅在后续传播贡献较大;但2024–2025鹅源病毒仅5.9%(3/51),可能反映鹅死亡或尸体检测减少;鹭源病毒43.13%(22/51),明显高于既往HPAI暴发。2022–2023有鹤类(hooded cranes和white-naped cranes)大规模暴发,2024–2025未检出HPAI阳性鹤,显示受影响宿主季节差异。研究人员强调需继续多物种监测和野鸟病理生物学研究。野生鸟源病毒分析需谨慎,因监测可能不完全代表野鸟多样性,并偏向体型较大、数量较多或致病性较高物种。 研究结论翻译:总体而言,基于HA基因的贝叶斯系统发育动力学分析刻画了HPAI H5N1病毒进入韩国的多条引入途径、国内基因型特异性传播模式,以及野鸭和大白鹭在病毒传播中的关键作用。韩国多条引入途径和频繁重配事件的观察强调了该地区HPAI流行病学的复杂性。这些发现为HPAI H5N1病毒传播动力学提供了有价值的见解,并强调针对关键禽类宿主和高风险地理区域加强监测项目的重要性。然而,野生鸟源病毒分析应谨慎解释,因为监测可能不能完全代表野生鸟类种群多样性,并可能偏向体型较大、数量较多或表现出较高致病性的物种。未来整合病理生物学、野生鸟类生态学和候鸟迁徙数据的综合方法将进一步促进对野生鸟类中clade 2.3.4.4b HPAI生态学和流行病学的理解,并提高对未来暴发的准备。 论文解读:韩国野生鸟类中新型重配HPAI H5N1 clade 2.3.4.4b病毒的多重引入与传播(2024–2025)研究背景:A/Goose/Guangdong/1/1996(Gs/Gd)谱系H5高致病性禽流感(HPAI)病毒1996年在中国家鹅中首次发现,之后在家禽和野鸟中反复暴发。其血凝素(HA)基因已分化为0–9 clades及多个亚支。clade 2.3.4于2008年在中国出现,演化为2.3.4.4a–h。clade 2.3.4.4b自2014年起与当地禽流感病毒广泛重配,产生H5N2、H5N5、H5N6、H5N8等。至2021年末,clade 2.3.4.4b H5N1成为全球优势HPAI亚型,在亚洲、欧洲、非洲和北美野鸟和家禽中广泛暴发,并因宿主范围扩大和反复溢出至哺乳动物(包括人)引发公共卫生关注。韩国自2003年首次HPAI H5N1侵入后,反复发生H5Nx(H5N1、H5N8、H5N6)暴发,野鸟在引入和传播中持续起关键作用。2020–2021冬季报告234例野鸟clade 2.3.4.4b HPAI病例,2021–2022为47起,2022–2023为174例,2023–2024为19例,季节间规模和病毒活动差异明显,并出现不同基因型,提示反复引入和野鸟种群内重配。2024–2025季节clade 2.3.4.4b H5N1经野鸟传入韩国,且2025年3月在野生豹猫(Prionailurus bengalensis)中检测到HPAI H5N1,为韩国该野生哺乳动物首次记录的溢出事件,显示宿主范围扩大和对动物及公共卫生的持续威胁。因此,研究人员对2024–2025冬季韩国野鸟和豹猫H5N1病毒进行全基因组测序和比较系统发育分析,以阐明时空扩散路径、进化动态和宿主转换。研究人员开展:2024年10月14日至2025年3月24日,通过国家HPAI监测项目在野生鸟类中报告43例clade 2.3.4.4b HPAI H5N1感染,1例豹猫。研究人员对51株H5N1分离株进行全基因组测序,并开展系统发育和贝叶斯系统发育动力学分析。结论:鉴定出9种基因型Kor24–25A至Kor24–25I;Kor24–25A(n=24)最常见,在鹈形目频繁检出,2024年12月至2025年1月最流行;贝叶斯分析基于HA识别三条最可能引入路线,经京畿、济州和朝鲜半岛南部沿海;野鸭为主要储存宿主;大白鹭更频繁受累,提示季节性物种差异。意义:提供HPAI H5N1系统发育动力学、宿主关联和传播模式见解,强调针对关键禽类宿主和高风险地区加强监测。关键技术方法(不超过250字):样本来源为2024年10月至2025年3月由韩国国家野生动物疾病控制与预防研究所(NIWDC)开展的全国野动物监测,覆盖92个监测点,收集57,067份粪便和1,007份个体鸟拭子,并被动检查419具尸体;从43起暴发中获得51株病毒,包括2025年3月16日豹猫分离株。关键技术包括:实时逆转录聚合酶链反应(rRT-PCR)筛查M和H5基因;Illumina MiSeq全基因组测序并提交GISAID;MAFFT比对,RAxML最大似然(ML)系统发育,iTOL可视化,PopArt最小生成网络,ggFLU基因型分类,FluMut分子标记筛查;TempEst评估分子钟信号;BEAST/BEAUti贝叶斯连续和离散性状系统发育动力学分析,CIPRES运行,Tracer、LogCombiner、TreeAnnotator、FigTree、EvoLaps、SpreaD3和PACT分析传播路线、宿主转换和状态比例。研究结果:**Multiple genotypes of 2024–2025 HPAI viruses**:研究人员成功对2024年10月14日至2025年3月24日韩国野生动物中51株H5N1 HPAI病毒测序。ML系统发育显示所有基因片段存在多个遗传簇,bootstrap支持>90:PB2三个、PB1四个、PA三个、HA两个、NP五个、NA两个、M一个、NS一个。多数簇内序列>98%成对核苷酸同一性,但A/Mandarin transmission patterns of HPAI viruses**:基于HA的贝叶斯系统发育动力学揭示2024–2025冬季H5N1时空传播。cluster 1首例为2024年10月14日京畿地区捕获鸳鸯,HA来自Kor24–25A,tMRCA为2024年5月6日(95% HPD:2023年12月20日至2024年8月29日)。连续系统地理学提示京畿道为cluster species**:基于HA的宿主离散性状分析中,BF>3且PP>0.5为显著,BF>20且PP>0.9为强支持。cluster HPAI与共循环HPAI或LPAI频繁重配。内部基因中M和NS遗传多样性最低,NP形成最多单系枝。Kor24–25A、B、C为主要基因型,时间分布不同;A持续整个监测期,B仅早期至2024年12月第五周,约占采样期一半。早期基因型短暂优势此前已有,但与2021–2022和2022–2023冬季随暴发进展基因型多样性下降不同,本季持续遗传多样性。A、B、C在多种野鸟检出,宿主范围广。雁形目基因型多样性最高,符合其主要水鸟储存库角色;鹭至其他野鸟传播提示鹭可作易感宿主和潜在中间宿主参与本地传播。猛禽基因型多样性第二,与既往低或无禽流感病毒流行报道不同,可能因捕食感染水禽。豹猫Kor24–25C零星感染可能因食入感染野鸟。HA贝叶斯分析识别三个病毒簇,表明至少三次独立引入。cluster 1引入地区既往为HPAI侵入高风险区,来自俄罗斯、蒙古、中国风险高于日本;cluster 2引入地区济州和南部与日本引入风险较高,且2024–2025日本H5 HPAI HA分析显示来自cluster 2a的24WC025与同期日本分离株同枝。2a和2b可能经野鸟独立引入不同地区,所有基因序列与既往野鸟病毒近,重配更可能发生在亚洲迁徙野鸟而非家禽。离散性状分析提示季节特异性宿主分布和传播。既往研究认为野鸭在早期引入和国内传播重要,鹅在后续传播贡献较大;但2024–2025鹅源病毒仅5.9%(3/51),可能反映鹅死亡或尸体检测减少;鹭源病毒43.13%(22/51),明显高于既往HPAI暴发。2022–2023有鹤类(hooded cranes和white-naped cranes)大规模暴发,2024–2025未检出HPAI阳性鹤,显示受影响宿主季节差异。研究人员强调需继续多物种监测和野鸟病理生物学研究。野生鸟源病毒分析需谨慎,因监测可能不完全代表野鸟多样性,并偏向体型较大、数量较多或致病性较高物种。研究结论翻译:总体而言,基于HA基因的贝叶斯系统发育动力学分析刻画了HPAI H5N1病毒进入韩国的多条引入途径、国内基因型特异性传播模式,以及野鸭和大白鹭在病毒传播中的关键作用。韩国多条引入途径和频繁重配事件的观察强调了该地区HPAI流行病学的复杂性。这些发现为HPAI H5N1病毒传播动力学提供了有价值的见解,并强调针对关键禽类宿主和高风险地理区域加强监测项目的重要性。然而,野生鸟源病毒分析应谨慎解释,因为监测可能不能完全代表野生鸟类种群多样性,并可能偏向体型较大、数量较多或表现出较高致病性的物种。未来整合病理生物学、野生鸟类生态学和候鸟迁徙数据的综合方法将进一步促进对野生鸟类中clade 2.3.4.4b HPAI生态学和流行病学的理解,并提高对未来暴发的准备。 打赏 展开全文 开通VIP 享海量文章 全站开放获取+解锁超过 200万篇新闻全文+每日更新 2000+篇,学术前沿动态全时追踪! 从2000到2026,27载全因有您;您的每一分支持,都给予我们无穷的动力! 公测期:打赏8元解锁30天免费阅读!

由于禽流感,香港暂停从美国和英国地区进口家禽 - avinews.com

Hong Kong suspends poultry imports from areas in US and UK due to avian influenza - avinews.com
新闻简报香港于2026年10月5日暂停了从美国和英国特定地区进口家禽,原因是高致病性H5N1 禽流感的爆发。该决定立即生效,旨在保护香港的公共卫生。香港食物及环境卫生署的食品安全中心发布了此指令,指出2026年上半年从美国进口了约24,190吨家禽肉和20.2百万枚鸡蛋,从英国进口了340吨家禽肉。香港已联系美国和英国当局,并将监测世界动物卫生组织(WOAH)及相关机构的最新动态。食品安全中心表示,将根据事态发展采取适当措施。此措施是香港应对禽流感 暴发的一部分,该病毒是禽流感主题合同分析的主要候选对象。
查看新闻五要素
时间
香港于2026年10月5日暂停从美国和英国进口家禽,该决定立即生效。该新闻报道发布日期为2026年10月7日。
地点与对象
香港特别是食物环境卫生署辖下的食物安全中心(中心)实施了进口禁令。受影响的人群包括可能接触来自美国和英国的家禽产品的个人。
事件
由于暴发高致病性H5N1 禽流感,香港暂停从美国和英国特定地区进口家禽。这一行动由aviNews.com报道并得到中心证实。
规模、影响与风险
进口暂停影响了来自美国和英国的禽类肉类及其产品,包括鸡蛋。香港食物及卫生局引用了今年前六个月从美国进口约24,190吨禽类肉类和20.2百万枚鸡蛋,以及从英国进口340吨禽类肉类的数据。该风险被明确指出是对香港公众健康的威胁。
应对、状态与不确定性
香港已就禽流感 暴发事宜联系美国和英国当局,并将留意世界动物卫生组织(WOAH)和相关当局的最新消息。将根据事态发展采取适当行动。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Hong Kong suspends poultry imports from areas in US and UK due to avian influenza - avinews.com

News Brief
Hong Kong suspended poultry imports from specific areas in the US and UK on October 5, 2026, due to outbreaks of highly pathogenic H5N1 avian influenza. The decision, effective immediately, aims to protect public health in Hong Kong. The Centre for Food Safety (CFS) of Hong Kong's Food and Environmental Hygiene Department issued the directive, citing the import of about 24,190 tons of poultry meat and 20.2 million eggs from the US, and 340 tons of poultry meat from the UK in the first half of 2026. Hong Kong has contacted US and UK authorities and will monitor updates from the World Organization for Animal Health (WOAH) and relevant agencies. The CFS stated that appropriate actions will be taken based on the situation's development. This measure is part of Hong Kong's response to the avian influenza outbreak, which is a primary candidate for analysis under the avian influenza topic contract.
Original source text (English)

READ SUMMARY SUBSCRIBE RECEIVE IN PAPER OTHER EDITIONS Back Animal Health & PathologyMarketTrends Avian influenzaH5n1Poultry import ban 07 Oct 2026 Hong Kong suspends poultry imports from areas in US and UK due to avian influenza Hong Kong has contacted the American and British authorities over the issues and will closely monitor information issued by the WOAH. aviNews Asia SAVE IN THE LIBRARY PDF FILE The Centre for Food Safety (CFS) of Hong Kong’s Food and Environmental Hygiene Department on October 5 instructed the trade to suspend the import of poultry meat and products, including poultry eggs, due to outbreaks of highly pathogenic H5N1 avian influenza in areas in the US and the UK with immediate effect to protect public health in Hong Kong. The areas include Big Stone County and Kandiyohi County of the State of Minnesota, Ravalli County of the State of Montana, and Barron County of the State of Wisconsin in the US, and in the Isle of Lewis of Scotland in the UK, according to notifications from the World Organization for Animal Health (WOAH). A CFS spokesman said that according to the Census and Statistics Department, Hong Kong imported about 24,190 tons of chilled and frozen poultry meat and about 20.2 million poultry eggs from the US, and about 340 tons of chilled and frozen poultry meat from the UK in the first six months of this year. “The CFS has contacted the American and British authorities over the issues and will closely monitor information issued by the WOAH and the relevant authorities on the avian influenza outbreaks. Appropriate action will be taken in response to the development of the situation,” the spokesman said. B 常规重要公共卫生新闻

冬季和候鸟引发新的 禽流感 病例,爱尔兰进入高风险期

Ireland enters higher-risk period as winter and migrating birds raise fears of fresh avian influenza cases
新闻简报Ireland is entering a high-risk period for avian influenza as winter conditions and the arrival of migratory birds increase the likelihood of the disease spreading. The Department of Agriculture, Food and the Marine (DAFM) has confirmed that no wild birds have tested positive for avian influenza in recent months, but the risk is rising due to colder weather and shorter daylight hours, which can extend the survival time of the virus in the environment. The DAFM is urging bird keepers, including commercial poultry farmers and backyard bird owners, to implement strict biosecurity measures to prevent the disease from entering their premises. Authorities are also encouraging the public to report suspected cases through the Avian Check app or the National Disease Emergency Hotline. While the exact number of cases in Ireland is not specified, the disease remains a threat to the poultry industry, with potential for outbreaks that could lead to restrictions on bird movements. The EU has recently announced €73 million in agricultural reserve funding for Italy to assist farmers affected by avian influenza and African swine fever, highlighting the broader impact of the disease on the agricultural sector.
查看新闻五要素
时间
爱尔兰正进入 禽流感 的高风险期,因为寒冷的天气和候鸟增加了该疾病到达该国的威胁。该发言人指出,冬季即将到来,这种疾病仍然对家禽生产者构成威胁。
地点与对象
爱尔兰,重点关注家禽生产者,包括商业家禽养殖场、后院饲养员和圈养鸟类的饲养员。
事件
由于天气寒冷和候鸟迁徙,爱尔兰正进入 禽流感 的高风险期。农业、食品和海洋部 (DAFM) 已确认近几个月没有 野生鸟类 检测呈阳性,但风险正在增加。
规模、影响与风险
该疾病仍然对家禽生产者构成威胁,暴发 有可能导致限制鸟类活动和采取其他措施以防止进一步传播。欧盟已宣布为受影响的农民提供资金,但证据并未量化对爱尔兰的具体影响。
应对、状态与不确定性
农业部敦促鸟类饲养者保持严格的生物安全措施,并通过禽类检查应用程序或国家疾病紧急热线报告可疑病例。目前正在积极监测和评估局势。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Ireland enters higher-risk period as winter and migrating birds raise fears of fresh avian influenza cases

News Brief
Ireland is entering a high-risk period for avian influenza as winter conditions and the arrival of migratory birds increase the likelihood of the disease spreading. The Department of Agriculture, Food and the Marine (DAFM) has confirmed that no wild birds have tested positive for avian influenza in recent months, but the risk is rising due to colder weather and shorter daylight hours, which can extend the survival time of the virus in the environment. The DAFM is urging bird keepers, including commercial poultry farmers and backyard bird owners, to implement strict biosecurity measures to prevent the disease from entering their premises. Authorities are also encouraging the public to report suspected cases through the Avian Check app or the National Disease Emergency Hotline. While the exact number of cases in Ireland is not specified, the disease remains a threat to the poultry industry, with potential for outbreaks that could lead to restrictions on bird movements. The EU has recently announced €73 million in agricultural reserve funding for Italy to assist farmers affected by avian influenza and African swine fever, highlighting the broader impact of the disease on the agricultural sector.
Original source text (English)
IRELAND is entering a high-risk period for avian influenza as cold weather and migrating birds increase the threat of the disease reaching the country. The Department of Agriculture, Food and the Marine (DAFM) has confirmed that no wild birds have tested positive for avian influenza in Ireland in recent months. Ireland is now entering a high-risk period for bird flu Credit: DHUB_Search_Alamy_NUK This is due to cold weather and migrating birds increasing the threat of the disease Credit: Owen Humphreys/PA Wire Just two cases of the disease have been recorded in Ireland so far this year. DAFM said the combination of winter conditions and bird migration patterns means the coming months represent a higher-risk period for avian influenza entering Ireland. Sign up to The Irish Sun’s daily newsletter Thank you! A spokesperson said: “As the winter begins to set in, weather conditions and bird migration routes bring a higher risk period for incursion of avian influenza to Ireland. “Colder weather and shorter daylight hours can extend the survival time of avian influenza viruses in the environment, and migratory wild birds (the natural hosts of many avian influenza viruses) arrive in large numbers to overwinter in Ireland.” The department is now urging anyone keeping captive birds to maintain strict biosecurity measures to reduce the risk of infection. DAFM has stressed that strong biosecurity is the most important measure bird owners can take to prevent diseases entering their premises and spreading among poultry. Bird keepers are also being urged to report suspected cases to the authorities through the Department of Agriculture’s Avian Check app or the National Disease Emergency Hotline on 01 492 8026 . The spokesperson added: “All commercial poultry farmers, backyard keepers and those with captive birds should take action to reduce the risk of their flocks becoming infected, for the protection of the Irish poultry industry. Most read in The Irish Sun “Members of the public are advised not to handle sick or dead wild birds they come across.” The warning comes after detections of highly pathogenic avian influenza in birds continued to fall across the EU , following a decline that had already been recorded during the spring. Despite the lower number of cases, the disease remains a threat to poultry producers. This is because outbreaks can lead to restrictions on bird movements and other measures designed to prevent further spread. The EU has recently announced €73million in agricultural reserve funding for Italy to help farmers affected by outbreaks of avian influenza and African swine fever. Of that amount, €61million will be directed towards poultry and egg producers who were impacted by avian influenza outbreaks between September 2022 and March 2025. The funding is intended to help compensate farmers for financial losses caused by measures introduced to contain and prevent the spread of the diseases. France has also faced a major impact from avian influenza, with the country experiencing a major resurgence of highly pathogenic cases during the 2025-2026 epidemiological season. A total of 156 outbreaks were recorded in France during the season, including 125 on commercial poultry farms. Authorities introduced a range of sanitary measures to limit transmission, including restrictions on the movement of poultry within areas subject to disease controls. The measures had financial repercussions throughout the poultry sector, affecting businesses beyond farms where outbreaks were recorded. French Agriculture Minister Annie Genevard has since announced a €10million financial assistance scheme for businesses involved in hatchery operations, collection and downstream activities
View five news elements
Time
Ireland is entering a high-risk period for avian influenza as cold weather and migrating birds increase the threat of the disease reaching the country. The spokesperson noted that the winter is beginning to set in, and the disease remains a threat to poultry producers.
Location and population
Ireland, with a focus on poultry producers, including commercial poultry farms, backyard keepers, and those with captive birds.
Event
Ireland is entering a high-risk period for avian influenza due to cold weather and migrating birds. The Department of Agriculture, Food and the Marine (DAFM) has confirmed no wild birds have tested positive for avian influenza in recent months, but the risk is increasing.
Scale, impact and risk
The disease remains a threat to poultry producers, with the potential for outbreaks leading to restrictions on bird movements and other measures to prevent further spread. The EU has announced funding for affected farmers, but the specific impact on Ireland is not quantified in the evidence.
Response, status and uncertainty
The Department of Agriculture is urging bird keepers to maintain strict biosecurity measures and report suspected cases through the Avian Check app or the National Disease Emergency Hotline. The situation is under active monitoring and assessment.
